L Version history

Version 2.6, 2 November 2026. The main argument now follows the decision a regulator would face: the evidence for a safeguard, the scope of a possible obligation and the response to failure. Detailed treaty machinery is arranged in reference appendices. The nine failure conditions and twelve monitoring clocks are tables. The full earlier version remains available in the DOI record's file history.

The opening and the detailed account distinguish behavioural persistence from a hardware-enforcement property. The paper states the pilot result within its task and performance limits, separates a policy rule from a mathematical theorem, and makes the unresolved suspension procedure visible. The glossary clarifies that a hash commitment alone does not establish confidentiality. No treaty article, registered proposition or experimental result changes.

The seven versions of this paper are dated as follows: version 1, 29 September 2026; versions 2 and 2.1, 1 October 2026; version 2.2, 3 October 2026; version 2.3, 5 October 2026; version 2.4, 8 October 2026; and version 2.5, 1 November 2026. The notes below, newest first, say what changed in each version after the first.

What changed in version 2.5

  • The treaty's certification is named only as the Eden Seal, its name since 27 September 2026. Section 3.3 quotes only the part of the book's sentence on the certification that does not carry the book's earlier name for it, and section I.3.1 quotes the book's novelty claim up to the sentence that carries it and gives the rest of that claim in my words. Sections 4.5 and 12.1 and Table 9 speak of the change of name without the earlier one.

  • The record is stated as it stands on 1 November 2026 wherever the paper states it as current. Laws IV and V were registered as laws on 15 October 2026, in version 1.103 of the registration, so all five laws are registered, each a law under test (abstract and sections A.1, A.1.2, B.2.2, D.2.8, E.2.3, E.3.2, H.1 and 9), and their mathematics, which the paper cited at version 1.0 of 3 October 2026, is cited by its DOI as in development. Unit 83 ran on 27 and 28 October 2026: its result on P21 is stated with its scope wherever the paper gives a verdict, and in short form elsewhere (abstract and sections A.1, A.1.1, A.1.2, B.1.3, D.2.7, D.2.8, E.2.3, D.3.3, H.1, H.2.3 and H.2.9). Section 0.1 adds unit 06's registered run and unit 82's registration, and the matrix unit 06's run.

  • Quotations are brought to the present versions of the papers they cite. Where a cited version no longer carries the words quoted, the paper quotes what that version says on the same point or states the point in my own words: Paper X in sections E.2.3 and D.3.1, Paper XIII in section D.3.6, Paper III in sections A.1 and I.2.2, The ARC Theory in section B.1.2, Paper IV.d in section D.1.3, and the engineering specification's line on the chokepoint in section H.2.2, which now stands in my words. The references give Papers III, IV.b and XIII, The ARC Theory, Eden Engineering and the mathematics of Laws IV and V their present titles, and the registration its later versions.

  • The box in section D.3.1 is pinned to commit 83871b5 of the code repository, whose suite of 104 tests passes in its verification record; until this version it pinned commit 32eb3135 of 28 September 2026.

  • Sections 10.3 and 16.5 locate the engineering specification's monitoring removal test and its condition F-EDEN-4 in its version 8.1.

  • Section 23.7 and back matter B state the record of 8 December 2024 at its strength: its time is signed by the sending server's DKIM signature and sealed by Google's ARC set.

  • Section 15.2 cites the note on trust infrastructure by its title and authors, and names no funder.

  • Section 0.2's dated note of 3 October 2026 gives way to a note of 1 November 2026; the earlier note is kept whole below, as it then stood.

  • No registered law, prediction, kill condition, obligation or dependency changes, and no date of any claim moves.

The note of 3 October 2026, as section A.1.2 printed it until version 2.4:

This paper and Laws IV and V, 3 October 2026. The paragraphs above give the status of the two laws. This paper argues from them and tests neither. Section 0 sets out how its two layers follow from them, and the argument is conditional on the three propositions that carry them: none of the three has a result yet, and if they fail, my case that correction must be built in falls with them (section B.1.3). The reading that correction formed from the start does better than correction added later is mine: P12 registers no direction, and no registered proposition states it. This paper reports no measurement of its own on timing, on placement or on persistence. Where it cites results of the programme's other papers, none is a deciding test of P15, P12 or P21. Until this version the paper said that the mathematics of the two laws was in development, and section B.2.2 cited one draft of it. The mathematics is now set out at version 1.0 of 3 October 2026, and the paper cites that version. Nothing that any duty depends on changes with it: the treaty's obligations and the conditions that arm them stand as they were.

What changed in version 2.4

  • The paper now opens in the programme's standard form: an abstract under its own heading, with keywords, and a statement of what the paper commits to, drawn from its own sections. The abstract adds two sentences: that the case for the treaty is argued from the laws of the ARC Theory and the methods of the Eden Protocol, its two layers following Laws IV and V, and that ARC-Align does not yet measure either exponent in the criterion. The line describing the treaty now stands under the paper's title, before the author line, and the author line gives my title as founder of Recursive Dynamics. The notes of what changed in each version, which stood at the head of the paper, now stand here, after a line giving the date of each version, in the same words, save the one change to the note on version 2.3 listed below; the tables carry numbered captions; and back matter C now says that the disclosure follows the version history.

  • Statements of the status of Laws IV and V now use the same clauses: their propositions registered, and neither law yet registered as a law or tested. Every mention of the mathematics of the two laws, set out at version 1.0 of 3 October 2026, now links to its DOI, and its reference entry, which said that it carried no DOI, now gives the DOI. Section 1.1 now states Law V by the two propositions that carry it, on placement and on build order, and no longer gives it the direction that section A.1 marks as my own reading.

  • Section 16.5 and the reference entry for Eden Engineering said that its abstract places the monitoring removal test at Section 7. Its abstract names no section, and the test that section E.3.5 cites is the specification's falsification condition F-EDEN-4, in its Section 8; both now say so. Section 10.3 described the monitoring removal test as asking whether safety sits in the weights or only in the wrapper; it now describes the test in the specification's words and cites its Section 7.

  • Sections 0.1, 8.7 and 8.8 now record that the corrector-class audit, unit 06, was registered on OSF on 1 October 2026 and became public on 4 October 2026, and that it has not been run. Section 0.1 and the matrix add that unit 06's own registration writes the mapping to P7's three verdicts that the registration had left unwritten, and the matrix adds that, on the floors unit 06 registers, no verdict on P7 can stand. In the matrix, the rows for P7, P12, P13, P15, P19 and P21 now name the parts the registration gives them in the Eden Protocol: its premise, its four carriers and its motivation.

  • Section 4.1 said that every passage quoted from my book was taken from the served text, which carries corrections of 17 January 2026, and that this text had not been compared line by line against the printed interior. It now says that every passage of the book quoted here is checked against the ebook edition of 6 January 2026 and credited to that edition, because the printed first edition differs from it slightly in places; the reference entry for the book names that edition, and three passages, one of them quoted twice, now carry the ebook's own quotation marks and apostrophes. It also says that the manuscripts of 8 December 2024 and 30 April 2025 have been public on OSF since 5 October 2026, while the emails are held privately for now.

  • Section 15.1 quoted the background paper of the International Atomic Energy Agency with one word in a form that paper does not use; the quotation now has that paper's own words.

  • More mentions of the works this paper cites are linked: the Draft Instruments to their DOI, and further mentions of treaties and documents to their official sources. The Geneva Conventions and Protocol I now link to their texts in the United Nations Treaty Series, volumes 75 and 1125. The reference entry for the registration gives its registered title, and the reference list's preface says which entries give a version and that only the entry for my book links to a page of my own website.

  • The note on version 2.3 no longer prints the word it withdrew from the statements of Laws IV and V.

  • No registered law, prediction, kill condition or dependency changes; no result is added; no date of any claim moves.

What changed in version 2.3

  • The title is now Treaty Paper · The HARI Treaty · Hardware to Buy Time, Architecture to Last, in the programme's uniform order (paper, its law or framework, its own title); until this version it read The HARI Treaty: hardware to buy time, architecture to last.

  • Laws IV and V are now stated as laws throughout: section A.1.2 now says that the theory has five laws, three registered and two added later, and five other passages drop the qualifying word. The laws' status is unchanged.

  • Mentions of the programme's papers in this paper now carry their uniform titles.

  • Eden Engineering is now cited at version 7.0, the Operational Definitions at version 1.9.1 and the Draft Instruments at version 2.3, the versions that carry their uniform titles.

  • The entry for the Operational Definitions gives their DOI, which the programme's catalogue records, where it said that the catalogue recorded none.

  • No registered law, prediction, kill condition or dependency changes; no result is added; no date of any claim moves.

What changed in version 2.2

  • The mathematics of Laws IV and V is now cited at version 1.0 of 3 October 2026. Until this version the paper said that the mathematics was in development, and section B.2.2 cited one draft of it (sections A.1, A.1.2, B.2.2, D.2.8, E.2.3, E.3.2 and H.1).

  • A dated note in section A.1.2 says what this paper does with Laws IV and V and what it does not decide: it argues from them, tests neither, and reports no measurement of its own on timing, on placement or on persistence.

  • Sections 0.1 and 8.7 record that one study unit, the corrector-class audit, was submitted for registration on OSF on 1 October 2026 and was not yet public when read on 3 October 2026.

  • Sections 4.2 and 11.3 called the results of Paper IV.c exploratory. The programme's dated ledger of its runs does not call that run exploratory, and the two sentences now say results from a single run.

  • More mentions of the works this paper cites are linked to their official sources. The title of my book is linked to the book's own page. The reference entry for the book gives its full title and says what that link is, and a new entry gives the mathematics of Laws IV and V.

  • The working-draft label is made exact. It now says that the review so far is the programme's own drafting review and not a review by a qualified lawyer, and which stages are still to run.

  • The paper now has an abstract under its title, and the description this page carries for search and citation tools is the same text.

  • Section 0 now says that each of the seventeen Annexes of the Draft Instruments is a description of what the Annex is to contain, and that thirteen of the fields in square brackets have no proposed default. Sections 4.4A and 6.5 said that every figure carries a proposed default; both now except those thirteen.

  • Sections 0 and 26 now say, beside the standard, that ARC-Align does not yet measure either exponent in the criterion (section D.3.3), and section A.1 gives the registration's own clause on what the ARC Bound bounds.

  • Section 8.6 now reports that the Draft Instruments hold two rules for how a duty ends, and gives both.

  • Section 21.4 now prints the financial terms the Draft Instruments hold and says that no budget estimate exists. One statement of cost there and one of scale in section C.4.1 are marked as my own estimates.

  • Section 22.5 said that the interim obligation of a signatory had no article of the Vienna Convention on the Law of Treaties as its model, and that neither instrument behind the withdrawal clause provides for revoking a notice. The obligation follows Article 18 of that Convention, and the revocation restates its Article 68; both sentences now say so, and the second names the two withdrawal clauses.

  • Statements about other bodies are made exact. Section 7.2 no longer calls the sentence of the United Nations scientific panel a finding, and says that its brief is an advance unedited version. Section 22.1 and its reference entry no longer say who launched the Call for Control of Frontier AI Models, and give the number of leaders its page listed when read on 3 October 2026. Section 22.3 gives the White House record's own word, globalist, marks its quotation as part of a sentence, and no longer says that nothing in the instrument is a scheme of control. Section 18.2 quotes the Verification Annex of the Chemical Weapons Convention where it paraphrased. Section 23.4 keeps its reservation for the Artificial Intelligence Act alone, and gives the Articles of the Council of Europe Convention on which its statement about that Convention rests.

  • Two statements that named no source are removed: a second lithography route (section C.1.2), and that one of my conditions had already fired (section H.2.5).

  • Correction on patents. Versions 1, 2 and 2.1 said that a patent application of mine was pending. I have filed no patent application; I have drafted a patent specification. Sections 0.4, 12.4 and 20.3 now say so.

  • Section 0 now says that a software model of the three-state gate exists and passes all 48 of its tests, beside the statement, which stands, that the hardware the chip chapter depends on has not been built.

  • Smaller corrections. The reading guide in section A.1 says an hour where it said twenty minutes, and the heading of section G.1 says in brief where it said on one page. Sections 4.1 and 23.7 gave 30 September 2026 as the date of this paper's second version; its deposit is dated 1 October 2026, and both now say so. Section 0.1 gives the day on which the replication register was read. The reference list's preface names the two entries that link to pages of my own website, no longer says that the catalogue records no version for the engineering specification, and loses four reserved entries from its last list. The Draft Instruments are cited at version 2.2.

  • No registered law, prediction, kill condition or dependency changes; no result is added; no date of any claim moves except the date of this paper's second version, corrected above.

What changed in version 2.1

  • Section 16.3 states what happens if the halt comes before covered chips reach stage S2 (Article 33(1A)).

  • Registering a covered agent deployment, and keeping logs of its agents' actions, are now part of the precautionary baseline (Article 28B(3)).

  • The Thresholds and Standards Committee decides, with reasons, within ninety days and subject to appeal, whether a scorer has passed the measurement gate, the first version of ARC-Align included. No scorer passes because no decision was made (Article 20(3)).

  • Every stage the paper names now says whose stage it is.

  • One universal claim and four superlatives with no census behind them are narrowed to what the record shows.

  • The references say why the book is not linked, and why two entries print one author's name differently.

What changed in version 2

  • The title now states the treaty's aim: hardware to buy time, architecture to last.

  • A new section, 4.4A, records my decisions of 29 and 30 September 2026, each with its date.

  • The treaty is set out as one treaty in two layers, the hardware layer and the architecture layer, carried by two integral protocols that enter into force with it (sections G.1 and E.1).

  • My standard is stated in my own words, with ARC-Align named as its test instrument. The standard binds only once its evidence stands, and no number from ARC-Align counts until it passes the treaty's measurement gate.

  • A duty now takes effect, and ends, only by a signed decision of the organ the treaty names, so no duty switches off without a decision (section D.2.6).

  • The source corrections made since version 1 are applied, and references link to their official sources. The check of the new links is still under way.
